<meta>This page begins the third book of the treatise, introducing a letter attributed to Alexander the Great. It establishes the foundational theory of the "microcosm" and the four natural virtues as they relate to the alchemical art.</meta>

### THE EPISTLE BEGINS
->Of the Emperor Alexander, who<-
->first reigned in Greece<-
->and Macedonia,<-
->and Emperor of<-
->the Persians.<-

Some say briefly that this part is derived from the creation of man, in whom all things are present. Just as the minor world <note>original: "minor mundus"; the Microcosm, or the human being as a reflection of the universe</note> was created from four different combined virtues, so also is this art created from the natures of woman. Some of these we briefly narrate, demonstrate, and set down, because the greater world <note>the Macrocosm or the universe</note> was created from four different and combined virtues and natures. The first of these is the appetitive <note>the power of seeking or desiring</note>, the second is the digestive, the third is the attractive, and the fourth is the expulsive, which is the cleansing power. The fifth is a foreign force, namely life, which joins these four virtues together that I have narrated and described to you, and whose arrangement I have explained. It is like a key to a chest: if you open it with the key, the whole is dissolved. For it is like a vessel containing its sides. This art is not perfected except by those who study with vigilance and through its temperance. Since (as I narrated before) the beginning of its action is from four performing virtues: namely the appetitive, the retentive, the containing digestive, and the expulsive, which is the cleansing power. The fifth is namely the soul, most bright from the light. Now, however, let us narrate these

<column-break/>

arrangements of the species; let us therefore explain it and its power. Let us narrate, then, which of them is hot, which is cold, which is masculine, and which is feminine, so that we do not err or go astray through the diversity of their colors and the difference of their kind. For if there is a difference between hot and cold, and masculine and feminine, the work will by no means be perfected for you unless it is directed on the right path. And you should know that no one born is born except from a male and a female, and no seed is produced except by moisture and heat. It is fitting for you to explain the forces. For the appetitive virtues are dry and masculine; they are hot and dry, and they are fire. The containing and retentive virtues are likewise masculine; they are cold and dry, and they are earth. The cleansing virtues are the digestive ones—namely the altering and feminine ones—and they are hot and moist, and they are air. The expulsive virtues are feminine; they are cold and moist, and they are water. The fifth is life, which is unique: it is not hot, nor moist, nor cold, nor dry, nor male, nor female. Therefore, do not seek it in any river-like place and err from the operation; I will show you its location. If, therefore, you wish to dissolve the virtue of cold water, you coagulate it more. If you wish to make cold water, you heat it more; unless you correct each nature of the virtue according to the purpose of what you are working on, you corrupt the work. Furthermore, because the appetitive forces are dry and coloring—that is, masculine—they are hot and dry, as was said, which affirm the color so that they are not separated or altered.

<summary>This page introduces an alchemical letter attributed to Alexander the Great, explaining that the "Art" mirrors the human body (the microcosm) through four primary virtues—appetitive, digestive, attractive, and expulsive—governed by a fifth vital force or soul.</summary>
<keywords>Alexander the Great, Microcosm, Four Virtues, Alchemy, Masculine and Feminine principles, Elements, Vital Force</keywords>

--- Page 116 ---
<meta>The previous page likely discussed the initial separation of elements; this page continues by defining the specific functional virtues (retentive, digestive, expulsive) and their elemental correspondences within the "lesser world" or microcosm of the human body and the alchemical work.</meta>

# BOOK

The **retentive** <note>original: *retentiuæ*; the power to hold or bind</note> virtues are feminine and dry: they gather color in bodies, soften them, and are joined to colors. The **digestive** virtue, however, is a masculine cleansing force which purifies the redness of the body and separates from it any accidental corruption of nature; it is hot and moist, feminine <note>The text here paradoxically assigns both masculine and feminine qualities to the digestive process</note>, and is associated with **Air**. The **expulsive** virtue, which is the cleansing of life itself, casts out the "earth" from itself until the parts are neither separated nor divided; just as a flaw is processed within the body, so the Art composes and gathers these virtues and their natures. This virtue is cold and moist: and it is **Water**. The true force that permeates the whole body is like the spirit of the body.

Now I shall name the species for you by their names and their "caverns" <note>likely referring to their internal vessels or anatomical seats</note>; for many have erred regarding many species, interposing useless things and leaving behind the useful. Some also, though working along the direct path as we commanded, diminished the work and acted according to their own opinions; therefore the work was ruined and turned to stone. Rather, I declare to you that if you desire to perfect your work, begin as I have commanded: and know that you work upon nothing else but those things which are of a lucid nature, from which the **microcosm** <note>original: *minor mundus*; the "lesser world" or human being as a reflection of the universe</note> was created, which I shall name for you by its properties. Know that **sulfur** is Fire, and **magnesia** <note>In alchemy, magnesia refers to a crude mineral substance or the "mother" of metals, not modern magnesium</note> is Earth: the sediment is Air: and **quicksilver** <note>original: *argentum vivum*; mercury</note> is the Water flowing through the body. The Spirit is also the "fecund water" by which all things are nourished, every bud is quickened, every light ascends, and which bears all fruit. If, therefore, you wish to perfect your work and

<column-break/>

best achieve those things you seek, take these two natures, namely the **male** and the **female**, the hot and the cold, the moist and the dry. Then nourish them with the **water of life** <note>original: *aqua vitæ*</note>, which possesses light and spirit. The work is likewise prepared equally by a robust essence and composition, and by the substance from which it is composed; for it is cast and composed from diverse natures. From **black bile** <note>original: *melancolia*</note> the body and its mass are made. The Fire, which is placed from **red bile** <note>original: *colera rubea*; yellow bile/choler</note>, is joined to it. Then comes that digestion which proceeds from the stomach: it is the water expelled into the liver, to which is joined the Air, which is the **blood** flowing in all the veins of the body; its heat increases until it turns to a red color. The water which is **phlegm** is placed in the chest, and it appears prominently in cold actions.

Then, from these things which I have named for you, the **physical body** <note>original: *corpus phisicum*</note> exists by divine virtue. Join these natures and guide them well: place each one within its own "house," and with its like or its contrary, so that the disposition may be perfected for you and the work completed. Just as I have granted to you through my grace, I have notified you how many species there are that act step-by-step until the body and spirit are made one. Then the wisdom of the Art joins them so that nature may be altered and converted into another nature through a nature that is fit; and then, having distinguished the natures from their accidental virtues, join the two natures. I will teach you to draw out the natures so they become lucid and most clear, from which the light ascends or shines and illuminates; after which, in the highest place, it may be adapted to dye <note>original: *tingat*; to tint or transmute</note>.

<summary>This page explains the alchemical "microcosm" by mapping the four humors (black bile, red bile, blood, phlegm) and the four elements to the internal functions of the body and the chemical process. It emphasizes the need to balance masculine and feminine qualities to achieve a "lucid" spiritualized body.</summary>

# BOOK THREE

You should know that I have composed this book of mine only for philosophers <note>original: "philosophis"; in this context, it refers to alchemists or seekers of natural wisdom</note>, in which I have enumerated the nature, the fitting together, and the described and revealed qualities of the species. It is necessary, moreover, for you to preserve well the works that are to be liquefied: and when you work with them, do not pass beyond the proper limit: for then all these secrets will appear to you: because liquefaction is the alteration of all natures. Hold to this, therefore, because they are mixed by nature into nature, until they arrive at their own substance, neither fleeing nor entering, neither altering nor varying: from which, once entered, the forced and smaller parts are distinguished. And then you shall know, because nature appears through nature. Liquefy the permanence of these fixed natures, then fit together the secrets, which are tempered and moist: because unless you thin the natures and make them into water, they will not be able to take on color. Thus, therefore, moisten the body by making it watery: since this water is called the **water of wisdom** <note>original: "aqua sapientiae"</note> among the philosophers. And know that whiteness <note>original: "albedo"; a key stage in the alchemical Great Work representing purification</note> is found in the joining together: and that the species are spirits, and they improve one another. If, therefore, you liquefy these species and then congeal them, you improve your work for the better. If you wish to perfect your work, see that you apply the species to it raw, not yet fitted together. Then you shall rot <note>original: "putrefacies"; refers to the process of breaking down matter through heat and moisture</note> the fitted species, raise them up, and let them settle. I will describe this arrangement openly to you, so that you may arrive at your work by ascending a direct path. And yet, you should then know that certain bodies require solution, and others require elevation: so that they may tint, and become bright and illuminated: in which all

<column-break/>

bodies may be dissolved. And know that, according to nature, secrets are hidden within them. But if they are governed by nature, the hidden things will appear to you, which appeared to the philosopher **Hermes** <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note>: and it is the white earth, nearly like silver <unclear>pernosar</unclear>. Prepare the bodies, therefore, when the earth is white, so that they remain fixed. Place a cover over it, lest the fire burn it with its flame: but let it melt by liquefying just like wax. But that which is called **instrumental water** is understood in your arts in which you study. There shall also be two in one, one of the **Sun** <note>gold</note>, the other of the **Moon** <note>silver</note>: then after these two, there are orders perceived by the eye, the intellect, and the heart. Behold, I have enumerated and revealed the celestial secret: I have illuminated a difficult and hidden science, and I have confirmed the perfect science of the method of governing. I swear, therefore, that I have concealed nothing, nor have I described any species with a foreign description: nor have I placed the types in foreign places. Therefore, you see what I have described: and you shall arrive at those things which I have described.

The example of our science is an old man beyond death; in him, the natures are joined with their completion: earth, water, his fire, and air. And all these are in Saturn <note>original: "Saturno"; in alchemy, Saturn represents both the metal lead and the initial, "black" stage of the process</note>. With him, the gates of the sciences are opened, as Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note> and his first predecessors say. Take the stone suspended above the sea, like an old man of white wax; over the fountain, like the child of the hypocist <note>original: "ypoquistidos"; a parasitic plant used in medieval medicine, here likely a symbolic reference to a specific substance or color</note>. Its name is Victor. With it, slay the living and bring life to the slain; for in it are death and life, both this and that, from the east and the west. With this, you shall have the knowledge in which the Arabs have grown weary. I have told you the truth by Him who caused the author of the stone of paradise, the light of life, to rise. In it are two opposites together, namely fire and water; this one gives life to that, and that one slays this, and both are joined together in the lion <note>The "Lion" often symbolizes the fixed sulfur or a strong acidic solvent</note>, by Him who is death and life. I have told the truth; I do not lie. Our gum coagulates our milk, and our milk dissolves our gum. And afterward, the oriental redness and the redness of blood will appear. This clean stone, without stench, is found in the highest places; with it, the philosophers have magnified themselves above all others and prophesied future things. I have told the truth by the power of the heavens, to which I have not knowingly lied. Our man is ancient; he is our dragon; he eats his own head with his tail <note>The Ouroboros, symbolizing the cyclical nature of the alchemical process and the unity of matter</note>. And the head and tail are the soul and the spirit; and the soul and spirit are created from clay; and both are from the east and the west, by Him who drives away all harm. I do not lie, because what I have said, I have seen

<column-break/>

in the city. Whiten your crow if you wish to whiten it. It becomes white first with the Nile of Egypt, then with Persia in secrets; and with both of these, a redness will appear like the poppy in the desert. By God who gives life to all things, I do not lie. Our water washes away filth; you shall cleanse all its blackness and darkness. Then it will smile, saying: "Who has cleansed me?" He who sees it will smile. I have spoken and told the truth; I do not lie. In the eyes of the earth there are three—east and west—and above them, white Saturn has risen; and the soul of these three is fire. I have told the truth, by the family of Muhammad <note>original: "familiam machometi"; a common oath in translated Arabic alchemical texts, suggesting the source's origin</note>. I will manifest what the first ones hidden, and not with envy. Our earth turns your copper <note>original: "as" (aes)</note> into silver first, and finally it is aged into gold, and you shall have the knowledge in which the Arabs have grown weary. You blacken with our Egypt, and not with Persia; and with Persia you dry, because it is the house of dryness; and with Egypt you moisten, because it is the house of moisture. And both of these are whiteness and redness without measure, like the redness in mulberries, or like the blood of ladies. The stone of our science is an old man at first, and a child at the last; because its whiteness is in the beginning, and its redness is at the end with dominion. This is the stone over which all who have gone away have stumbled, and many of them died with pain and sadness because they turned aside to the way of demons and left the way of prudence. The covering of the horse is our white cloak; and our horse is a strong lion covered under the cloak; and above both of them, our griffin <note>A mythical creature combining the eagle and lion, representing the union of the volatile and the fixed</note> retains a twin nature—

# THIRD BOOK

->K<-

...remaining. This stone is triangular in its essence: quadrangular in its quality. I have spoken the truth, through the operator whitening and reddening all things. These are not singular things in secret, but are mixed: East and West joined together by the soul of all things. This joins, and disjoins, and rejoins when it wills. Our wolf is found in the East: and the dog in the West. This one bit that one: and that one bit this one: and they both become rabid, and they kill each other, until from them is made a poison <note>original: "toxicum"</note> and a remedy <note>original: "tyriacha" – theriac, a universal medicine or antidote</note>. Our white camel is seventh in number among the great philosophers. The Sun with the Moon, Jupiter, Mars, and our Venus in our Mercury, and Saturn the seventh of them: in whom all are joined together. This is the blade, the sword, and the knife, and the carving tool upon the building, and the enemy at hand, and the vessel with the aid of wine. Then the stone in the running river, by the death of all I do not lie, is our example with the white of nature: fire and light in the essence of clarity. And fire and light is our spark, and our glowing firebrand: and with this and that, you shall find knowledge. I have spoken the truth through the one who unites all things. Our ladder is our tower, made of viscous earth: and viscous earth is our mud, and our waxen salve <note>original: "cerotum"</note>, made from wax and tallow: and here and there is our ship in our sea full of giants. I have opened what the ancients concealed. I am not envious as they were. By the one who illuminates all things, I do not lie.

<column-break/>

The earth placed before the eyes, East and West: and here and there the narcissus with our lily: and here and there with our old man: and our old man and our vessel <note>original: "vter" – a leather bottle or skin</note> is our body: and our body is our stone, sought by many, found by few. And this I have explained out of piety: and I have uncovered it to all through God the Begotten. Amen.

A certain king, wishing to overcome other powerful men, prepared himself for war: and when he wished to mount his horse, he commanded one of his soldiers to give him a drink of the water which he loved. The soldier said to him: "Lord, what is that water which you love?" The King said: "That is the water beloved by me, which loves me above all others." The soldier immediately understood, and brought it. The King drank and drank again, until all his limbs were filled, and even all his veins. And the King then became greatly discolored. This being done, the soldiers said: "Behold the horse, mount up." The King replied: "I cannot." The soldiers said: "Why can you not?" He replied: "Because I feel myself weighed down, and my head aches, and it seems to me: I ask and I will, that you place me in my bright chamber, and in a warm and tempered place, and continually through day and night, and within I shall sweat: and the water which I drank shall be dried up, and I shall be delivered." Which was done as he commanded. Then they opened the chamber and found him as if dead. His parents truly ran to the Egy... <note>The text cuts off mid-word, likely "Egyptians"</note>

# BOOK THREE

...remaining. This stone is triangular in its essence, but quadrangular in its quality. I have spoken the truth, through the operator who whitens and reddens all things. These are not solitary things in the hidden realm, but are mixed: East and West joined together by the soul of all things. This stone joins, disjoins, and rejoins whenever it wishes. Our wolf is found in the East, and the dog in the West. This one bit that one, and that one bit this one; and they both become rabid and kill each other in turn, until from them is made both poison and the remedy <term>theriac; an ancient herbal jam used as an antidote to poison</term>. And fire and light is our spark, and our glowing firebrand; and with this and that, you shall find knowledge. I have spoken the truth by the One who unites all things. Our ladder is our tower, made of viscous earth; and viscous earth is our mud, and our waxen salve <note>original: cerotum</note>, composed of wax and tallow; and here and there is our ship in our sea, full of giants. I have opened what the ancients hidden. I am not envious as they were. By the One who illuminates all things, I do not lie.

<column-break/>

The Earth is placed before your eyes, East and West: and here and there is the narcissus with our lily: and here and there with our old man: and our old man and our vessel <note>original: vter; literally a leather wine-skin</note> is our body: and our body is our stone, sought by many, found by few. And this I have explained out of piety, and I have uncovered it to all through God the Begotten. **Amen.**

A certain King, wishing to overcome other powerful men, prepared himself for war; and when he wished to mount his horse, he commanded one of his soldiers to give him a drink of the water which he loved. The soldier said to him: "Lord, what is that water which you love?" The King said: "That is the water beloved by me, which loves me above all others." The soldier immediately understood and brought it. The King drank and drank again, until all his limbs were filled, and even all his veins. And the King then became greatly discolored. This being done, the soldiers said: "Behold the horse, mount it." The King replied: "I cannot." The soldiers said: "Why can you not?" He replied: "Because I feel myself weighed down, and my head aches, and all my limbs are failing me, as it seems to me. I ask and desire that you place me in my bright chamber, in a warm and temperate place, continuously by day and night, and within I shall sweat; and the water which I drank shall be dried up, and I shall be delivered." This was done as he commanded. Then they opened the chamber and found him as if dead. ->Egyptians and Alexandrians<- <note>These represent two different schools of alchemical thought; the Egyptians represent the ancient, "older" tradition, while the Alexandrians represent the later, more refined Hellenistic methods.</note>, who were to be honored above all others, and they brought them in. They told them the whole story of what had happened to the king, saying: "Do you believe he can be freed from this distress?" They all answered: "Yes." And they asked: "Which of you is the master?" The Alexandrians answered: "We are, if it pleases you." But the Egyptians said: "On the contrary, we wish to be the masters in this matter, because we are older than you, even though we appear younger." The Alexandrians replied: "So be it." Then the said masters took him and divided him into tiny pieces <note>original: "membra minuta"; this describes the process of calcination or physical reduction of the metal</note>, and ground him down with their moistening medicines for a little while. They placed him, thus ground down, into his chamber in a warm and moist place, as before, and in the same manner through the day and night. Afterward, they drew him out as if dead, yet still possessing a tiny bit of voice. Then all his relatives cried out: "Alas, alas, he is dead!" But the physicians said: "He is not dead, but sleeps, and has not yet reached his end." They took him again and washed him with sweet water until the taste of the medicine departed; and they sent him back with just as much of that same empty medicine, and they did everything in the same manner and order as before. And when they drew him out, his relatives found him dead. Then the relatives said: "Why have you killed the king, when you were such great masters? Surely you seek evil upon yourselves." And those masters said: "Indeed, we have killed him so that he may become better and stronger on the day of judgment." <note>A reference to the alchemical "resurrection" where a substance is destroyed to be reborn in a purified state.</note> But the relatives then believed them to be swindlers, and they stripped them of their medicines

<column-break/>

and expelled them from the kingdom, saying afterward: "What shall we do with this body that is so poisoned?" Some answered: "Let us bury him underground, lest worms be bred from him and so that he does not rot, because the stench could harm us all." Then the Alexandrian masters said: "Do not bury him; for we will restore him to you healthier, more beautiful, stronger, and more powerful than he had been before." Then the relatives began to smile mockingly and said: "You wish to swindle us, just as the others swindled us. Wherefore know without a doubt that you shall not escape our hands as the others escaped, if you do not help him." The masters answered: "We agree; indeed, let us be put to death unless we do exactly as we have said." Then the Alexandrian masters, taking the body of the king, washed him and rinsed him well until nothing remained of the medicine of those others. Afterward, they dried him and took one part of "eagle" <note>term: "aquila" (eagle); in alchemy, this often refers to sal ammoniac or a volatile corrosive sublimate</note> and two parts of Alexandrian glass <note>likely a flux or a specific type of alkaline salt used in smelting</note>, and they put it with the dead body and made a paste with a little linseed oil. They placed him in a triangular chamber in the manner of a crucible, and left him for one hour in moderate heat. Afterward, they covered him with fire and blew with bellows until it was melted; and he descended through a hole into another vessel. Then the king arose robust, crowned with a royal diadem, and began to cry out: "Where are my enemies? Let all know that I shall put them to death unless they are obedient to me." Hearing this, they came immediately, saying: "Lord, command us whatever you wish, and we will do it at once." Then the king...

### HERE BEGINS THE BOOK
->of the Investigation of Mastery by Geber.<-

The investigation of this noble science, born from continuous and frequent diligence of work, and from excessive study in this labor, as well as from our deepest thoughts and various connections, I shall hand over to you with zeal: so that the following volumes may be better and more clearly understood by you; and so that once understood, and through ingenuity and scrutiny regarding them, they may be more easily and promptly brought to fruition. For it is one thing to investigate the theory of the art, and another to attempt and prove the subtleties and skills of these things: until, by working, scrutinizing, and experimenting, one arrives at the completed intent. In this book of mine, whatever we have investigated through the things narrated, we have written according to the reasoning of my mind: I speak of the things that perfect the art. Yet let no one think that this investigation, or our book, which is entitled *The Sum of Perfection of the Mastery* <note>original: "Summæ perfectionis magisterij," one of the most influential Latin alchemical texts</note>—

<column-break/>

—in which we have completely defined, according to the order of science, whatever true things we have seen and touched, according to experience and certain thought: which we have noted through our scrutiny of the various transforming effects of natural and mineral things appearing in the work. And we have endeavored to bring our previously composed science into agreement with this investigation, which we proposed by declaring it upon the work itself. Therefore, by right, this book precedes that one, since through this book one seeking to investigate the thought of the perfecting thing <note>the agent of transmutation</note> may do so: since this science treats the imperfect bodies of minerals, insofar as it is fitting to perfect them. In the first place, therefore, we have considered two things: namely, imperfection and perfection. Around these two we found our intention. Regarding the things that perfect and those that corrupt, according to what we have investigated through our experience, we compose this book: because opposites placed next to each other shine more brightly. Now, the thing which perfects in minerals is the substance of quicksilver <term>Argentum Viuum: mercury</term> and sulfur mixed proportionately, thickened and fixed by long and temperate decoction <note>slow heating</note> in the bowels of the clean earth, with the preservation of its radical moisture; not corrupting, but brought to a solid substance, fusible with proper ignition, and extensible under the hammer <note>malleable</note>. Through the definition of this perfecting nature, we can more easily arrive at the knowledge of the corrupting thing. And that is to be understood in the opposite sense, namely...

<summary>This page introduces the "Investigation of Mastery" by Geber, moving from a story about his miraculous physical relics to a scientific treatise on the composition of metals from mercury and sulfur. It establishes the foundational alchemical theory that metals grow in the earth through heat and can be perfected or corrupted.</summary>
<keywords>Geber, alchemy, quicksilver, sulfur, minerals, Sum of Perfection, investigation of mastery, transmutation, metallurgy</keywords>

-># BOOK<-

an impure substance of quicksilver <note>original: "argentum vivum"; mercury</note> and sulfur mixed without due proportion, either under-cooked or over-cooked in the bowels of the clean earth, neither rightly thickened nor fixed; having a combustible and corrupting humidity, and of a thin and porous substance; or having fusion without due ignition, or none at all; nor sufficiently enduring the hammer. I found the first definition <note>referring to the definition of perfection</note> embedded in these two bodies, namely in the Sun <note>gold</note> and the Moon <note>silver</note>, according to the perfection of each. But the second <note>the definition of imperfection</note> I found in these four: namely in tin, lead, copper, and iron, according to the imperfection of each. And because these imperfect bodies are not reducible to health and perfection unless the contrary is worked in them—that is, so that the manifest <note>the visible qualities</note> may be hidden, and the hidden <note>the internal essence</note> may be made manifest—which operation or contrariety is done through preparation: Therefore, preparation must be applied to them. Preparation is, therefore, to take away the superfluous, and to supply what is absent, and thus to place the mark of perfection in them. Indeed, perfect bodies do not lack this preparation. They do, however, require such a preparation so that their parts may be more refined, and reduced from their corporality to a fixed spirituality. The intention of which is to make from them a fixed spiritual body; that is, to attenuate and refine them much more than they were before. Concerning the preparations of all these, according to our investigation, we shall treat sufficiently in its proper place in this book. Which, after they have been sufficiently prepared, will be fit so that from them the great white or red elixir may be made.

<column-break/>

For we find that the moderns write to us of only one single stone for the complete white or red <note>the "White Work" for silver and the "Red Work" for gold</note>: which we concede to be true. For from whatever the white or red elixir is composed, there is nothing else there than quicksilver and sulfur; one of which without the other does nothing, nor can it exist. And therefore it is called "one stone" by the philosophers, although it may be extracted from many bodies or things. For to extract from a thing in which it is not, would be a foolish and vain thought, as some fools have believed; which was never the intention of the philosophers. They say many things, however, through metaphor. And because all metallic bodies are composed of quicksilver and sulfur—pure or impure, by accident, not innate in their primary nature—therefore it is also possible for these accidents to be removed through suitable preparation. For the stripping away of accidents is not impossible. Preparation is, therefore, to take away the superfluous and to supply the defect in imperfect bodies: which cannot be done without the assistance of the work and of purifying things. For preparation is diversified according to the diversity of the things requiring it. Experience has given us the modes of acting: namely calcination <note>reduction to powder by heat</note>, sublimation <note>vaporizing and solidifying</note>, descension <note>liquefying downward</note>, solution <note>dissolving</note>, distillation, coagulation, fixation <note>making a substance resistant to fire</note>, and ceration <note>softening to a wax-like state</note>. Concerning each of these, we have made a sufficient account in the *Sum of Perfection of the Magistery* <note>referring to the "Summa Perfectionis," a famous alchemical text attributed to Geber</note>. For these are the works assisting in preparation. The things assisting the preparations are these: namely all kinds of sa... <note>text cuts off; likely "salts"</note>

# BOOK THREE

...salts, alums, vitriols <note>original: "atramentorum," literally "inks," but in alchemy referring to vitriols or metallic sulfates</note>, and also glass, borax, and those things which are of this nature, as well as the sharpest vinegar and fire. And with these, we set out to prepare—that is, to cleanse—the imperfect bodies <note>The "imperfect bodies" refer to base metals like lead, tin, or copper, which alchemists sought to "perfect" into gold or silver.</note>: if any impurity should exist in them, according to our experience, in which we have become certain through the aforementioned means.

### The Cleansing of Common Salt.

Common salt <note>Sodium chloride</note> is cleansed in this manner. First, it is burned <note>calcined</note>; once burned, let it be dissolved in warm common water; once dissolved, let it be distilled through a filter; once distilled, let it be congealed over a slow fire in a glazed dish. Once congealed, let it be calcined for a day and a night in a moderate fire, and keep it thus sufficiently cleansed.

### The Cleansing of Alkali Salt.

Alkali salt <note>Potassium or sodium carbonate, often derived from plant ashes</note> is cleansed just like common salt; and it is the "fat of glass" <note>original: "sagimen vitri," a term for the scum or dross produced during glassmaking</note>. First, let it be ground and entirely dissolved in warm common water; afterwards, let it be distilled through a filter, congealed, and calcined with a slow fire.

### The Cleansing of Salt Gem.

First, let it be ground; then, let it be treated in the same way as common salt. <note>Salt gem refers to rock salt or halite, prized for its clarity.</note>

### The Cleansing of Sal Ammoniac.

First, let it be ground with the preparation of cleansed common salt. Afterward, let it be sublimated <note>The process of turning a solid into vapor and back to solid to purify it</note> in a tall sublimation vessel <term>aludel: a pear-shaped pot used in the sublimation process</term>, until it has been totally

<column-break/>

extracted pure. Afterward, let it be dissolved upon a porphyry stone, in the open air, if you wish to make a "water" <note>a liquid solution</note> from it; otherwise, keep it as it is, sublimated and sufficiently pure.

### The Cleansing of Other Salts.

Various other kinds of salts are still to be found, which are prepared and cleansed in the manner already described.

### The Cleansing of Alums.

First, concerning ice-alum <note>original: "alumine glaciali," likely clear potassium alum</note>. Many things can be prepared by means of these without any cleansing of the alum itself. However, it is cleansed in this manner: let it be placed in a distilling vessel <term>alembic: a chemical apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube, used for distillation</term>, and let all the moisture be extracted from it; this is of great value in this art. The dregs remaining in the bottom of the vessel may either be dissolved upon a stone, or in some damp place, or in the water extracted from it, or they may be set aside.

### The Cleansing of Yemen Alum.

Yemen alum <note>A specific variety of alum highly valued in the Middle Ages, originally imported from the East</note> is prepared just like ice-alum, except that it possesses greater power in this art.

### The Cleansing of Other Alums.

Many more alums are still found, all of which are prepared and cleansed as already stated.

### The Cleansing of Vitriols.

First, concerning black vitriol. The black is cleansed thus: first, let it be dissolved...

### The Cleansing of Copperas.

Copperas <note>original: "Cuperosa," likely copper sulfate or green vitriol</note> or Vitriol is cleansed in the same manner as black ink <note>original: "atramentum nigrum," referring to iron sulfates used in ink-making</note>. However, black ink possesses a greater earthiness than green ink.

### The Cleansing of Other Inks.

There are diverse inks, and diverse colors are found among them, which are cleansed as described above.

### Concerning Glass and Boraxes.

Glass and boraxes, if they have been made in the proper manner, do not require preparation.

manner. For these imperfect bodies possess superfluous moistures and a combustible sulfurousness which generates blackness when they are mixed, thereby corrupting the aforementioned bodies. They also possess an unclean, dreggy, and combustible earthiness—too coarse—which hinders penetration and fusion. These and such things are the superfluities in these aforementioned bodies, which have been discovered in them through our experience and certain, ingenious investigation. And because these superfluities come upon the bodies accidentally and not radically <note>meaning they are surface impurities, not part of the metal's "root" essence</note>, and the stripping away of accidents is possible, it behooves us—using artificial fire and these cleansed substances—to remove all accidental superfluities. Thus, only the substance of the quicksilver <note>mercury</note> and the radical sulfur remains. This constitutes the entire preparation of the imperfect [metals] and their perfect purification. The improvement, purification, and refinement of these, or of this remaining pure substance, is done in many ways, according to what the elixir <note>the Philosopher's Stone or transformative agent</note> requires. Therefore, the general method of preparation and purification is this: First, all superfluous and corrupt moisture in their essence must be raised up with a proportional fire, as well as the subtle and burning superfluity; and this is done by calcining. Then, the total remaining substance—corrupted in the lime <note>powder</note> of those bodies by the superfluous burning moisture and blackness—must be eroded away with these aforementioned cleansed corrosives, whether sharp or sour, until the lime becomes white or red, or colored according to the nature and property of the body, and is clean and pure from every

through the overflow or corruption previously described. And these things are cleansed with those corrosive agents by grinding, soaking, and washing. This is done by mixing and thoroughly grinding the aforementioned cleansed or pure substances—which do not possess a metallic melting point—with the aforementioned <term>lime</term><note>Latin: "calce"; here referring to a powdered metallic oxide produced by heating</note> purified in the manner described. In the melting or the reduction of the lime, these substances will retain the thick and unclean earthiness within themselves, while the body remains pure, cleansed of every corrupting superfluity. From this, the general method for the improvement and refinement of these pure substances is as follows: First, this purged and reduced body must be calcined again with fire and the aforementioned cleansing aids; then it must be dissolved with those things which are solvents. For this "water" is our stone, and <term>quicksilver</term><note>Latin: "argentum viuum"; mercury</note> from quicksilver, and <term>sulfur</term><note>Latin: "sulphur"; in alchemy, the active, combustible principle</note> from sulfur, abstracted and refined—or thinned—from the spiritual body. ### THE PREPARA-
### tion of Jupiter.

The preparation of Jupiter in particular. It is prepared in many ways; however, it is better done through this method. And it is thus: let it be placed in a calcining furnace in a vessel fit for this purpose, and let the fire be cast beneath it until the body itself reaches a good melting point. Then, stir the liquefied body with a perforated iron spatula, extracting the dross that is created upon it. Keep stirring in the heat of the fire, which should remain steady, until a good quantity of its powder has gathered on the surface. Let that be set aside, and stir again, until by turns the whole body has been reduced into a very fine powder. Then let that powder be sifted and placed back in the furnace, adding a good fire to it—not exceeding its melting point—and stirring it many times. Let it stand thus in the fire of its calcination for a natural day <note>A 24-hour period</note> or thereabouts. Finally, the total accidental moisture and superfluity will be destroyed, along with the combustible and corrupting sulfur. Afterwards, let the cleansed white lime be extracted. For the fire devours and consumes every fugitive and inflammable substance from the aforementioned things—namely, with cleansed common salt and alum.

And let it be washed with purified and very sharp vinegar: and let it be placed in the sun, or in the open air: and let it be ground again, and washed, and dried, and let this be repeated alternately in turn, until through the sharpness of the salts, the alums, and the vinegar, all its humidity, blackness, and impurity have been consumed, corroded, and destroyed. Then let crushed glass be added with these aforementioned things. And when the whole has been made into a paste, let it descend through a bearded vessel <note>original: "botum barbatum," a specialized distillation or filtration vessel used in alchemy</note> with sufficient fire. For the body descends pure and clean, while the entire earthy and dross-filled substance remains with the glass and the salts or alums. For in that pure body, descended and reduced, there is an equal and perfect proportion of pure quicksilver <note>mercury</note> and of white non-burning sulfur. For the fire and the corrosives have separated all the humidity and the fugitive, flammable, and corrupting substance, as well as the blackness; and through its descensory, or through the paste of salts, alums, and glass, the entire earthy dross substance has been separated, with the pure and tempered substance remaining in its proper proportion. Afterward, indeed, let this reduced pure body be calcined <term>calcined: reduced to a powder through heat</term> again with only pure, cleansed salt ammoniac. When it has been well and most minutely calcined, sublimated, and cleansed until it is of equal weight: then let the whole be ground very well and thoroughly spread upon a porphyry stone, and placed under the open sky in a cold and damp place: or in glass vessels in a dissolving furnace: or in horse dung <note>a common alchemical method for providing gentle, steady heat through fermentation</note>, until the whole has been dissolved, increasing the salt if it should be necessary. Which water...

...which we ought to honor. For it is this very thing which we seek for the White Work <note>the "albedo" or second stage of the Great Work, aimed at creating silver</note>. And let these things said in this final volume suffice concerning the preparation of Jupiter <note>the alchemical name for Tin</note>.

### ON THE PREPARATION of Saturn.

Saturn <note>the alchemical name for Lead</note> is prepared thus. Let it be placed similarly in a calcining furnace, stirring it as you did with Jupiter, until it is converted into a very fine powder. Let it be sifted and returned to the furnace. Let it stand in the fire of its calcination in the manner aforesaid, until the fugitive and flammable substance has been destroyed. Afterward, let the red lime <note>lead oxide or minium</note> be extracted: which should be soaked and ground very frequently and very finely with cleansed common salt, and vitriol <note>original: "atramento," likely iron sulfate</note>, and very sharp purified vinegar. Indeed, use these for the Red Work <note>the "rubedo" or final stage of the Great Work, aimed at creating gold</note>, just as you did for the White with common salt, Jamen alum <note>a specific type of alum, possibly from Yemen</note>, and vinegar. Then let it be dried, soaked, and ground many times as was done with Jupiter, until by the benefit of the said things, the said impurity has been destroyed. Then let glass be added with these aforementioned things, and let the pure body descend through the bearded vessel, being reduced as it descends. Again, let it be calcined with pure salt ammoniac, as with Jupiter: let it be ground very often, and dissolved in the manner aforesaid. For this is the water of quicksilver and sulfur, made proportionally: which we use in the composition of the Red Elixir. And let these things said concerning the preparation of Saturn suffice.

<summary>This page details the final purification of Tin (Jupiter) into a "pure water" for the White Work and outlines the calcination and distillation of Lead (Saturn) to create the Red Elixir.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Tin (Jupiter), Lead (Saturn), calcination, salt ammoniac, White Work (albedo), Red Work (rubedo), distillation, purification</keywords>

--- Page 129 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the purification of metals, following the previous discussions on chemical preparation. The catchword "me" from the previous page likely refers to the beginning of the process described here.</meta>

### ON THE PREPARA-
### tion of Venus <note>Venus is the alchemical name for Copper.</note>.

Copper is therefore best prepared in this manner. Let a layer of well-cleaned common salt <note>Sodium chloride</note> be placed in a crucible: and above it a plate [of the metal], and above that a layer of salt, and above that a plate, and so on in turns until the vessel is full. Let it be covered, sealed with clay <note>original: "lutetur", referring to 'lute', a paste used to make vessels airtight</note>, and placed in the calcination furnace for a natural day <note>A 24-hour period</note>. Let it be taken down and whatever has been calcined <note>Reduced to powder by heat</note> be scraped off: and let the plate be replaced with new salt. And so, by alternating turns, let them be calcined until all the plates have been consumed or corroded by the benefit of the salt and the fire. Because the salt corrodes the superfluous moisture and the combustible sulfurousness: the fire elevates the fugitive and inflammable substance in due proportion. Let that powder be ground altogether very finely, and washed with vinegar until the water flowing from it lacks any blackness. Imbibe and grind this in turns with new salt and vinegar: and after the grinding, let it be replaced in the calcination furnace in a suitable vessel, and let it stand there for three natural days. Then let it be extracted, well ground, refined, and washed with vinegar well and for a long time, until it has been cleaned and purged of all impurity. Let it be dried well in the sun. Then let half its weight of sal ammoniac <note>Ammonium chloride</note> be added, grinding well and for a very long time until the substance is impalpable <note>So fine it cannot be felt as distinct grains</note>. Place it under the open sky, or in the dung of resolution <note>A "manure bath," using the steady heat of fermenting dung to provide gentle, consistent warmth</note>, until whatever is refined there has been dissolved: renewing the cleaned sal

<column-break/>

ammoniac, if it should be necessary, until the whole becomes water. Honor this water, which we name the "water of fixed sulfur": with which the elixir is tinted even unto infinity. And let these things suffice concerning the preparation of Copper.

### ON THE PREPARA-
### tion of Mars <note>Mars is the alchemical name for Iron.</note>.

Iron is better prepared and calcined by this method, just as Copper was, with cleaned common salt, and let it be washed with pure vinegar. Once washed, let it be dried in the sun: once dried, let it be ground and imbibed again with new salt and vinegar, and placed in the same furnace for three days, as was done with Copper. Honor the solution, namely the water of fixed sulfur, which marvelously increases the color of the Elixir. And let these things said concerning the preparation of the imperfect bodies <note>The "base metals" like copper and iron, as opposed to the "perfect" noble metals</note> suffice.

Truly, the perfect bodies <note>Gold and Silver</note> do not lack preparation as far as perfection is concerned, since they are [already] perfect: but so that they may be further perfected, refined, and thinned: such a preparation must be applied. Let Sun <note>Sol is the alchemical name for Gold.</note> be taken, beaten into thin plates, and placed with well-prepared common salt layer upon layer <note>original: "solarfatim", a technical term for layering</note> in a vessel for cal-

<summary>This page provides detailed alchemical recipes for the calcination and purification of copper and iron using salt, vinegar, and heat to create a "water of fixed sulfur." It concludes by beginning the instructions for refining gold, the "perfect body."</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, copper, iron, gold, calcination, sal ammoniac, fixed sulfur, chemical purification, metallurgy</keywords>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the creation of the Elixir, moving from the preparation of "Sol" (gold) to "Luna" (silver) and discussing the philosophical requirements of the Great Work.</meta>

# BOOK
## XII

of calcination <note>original: "calcinationis"; the process of reducing a metal to a fine powder through heat</note>, and place it in the furnace, calcining it well for three days: or until the whole has been consumed and finely calcined. Then let it be taken out, ground very well, washed with vinegar, and dried in the Sun. Afterwards, let it be ground well with half its weight of cleansed salt ammoniac <note>ammonium chloride, used as a flux in alchemy</note>, and set to dissolve, until the whole has been dissolved into a most clear water through the benefit of the common salt and the salt ammoniac. This ferment is the perfect elixir, a precious and true spiritual body made manifest.

Moreover, silver <note>original: "argentum"</note> is refined and thinned, and reduced to a spiritual state through the method already described. Therefore, perform each and every step in that refinement just as you did for the Sun <note>original: "Sole"; gold</note>. And the water of dissolved Moon <note>original: "Lunae"; silver</note> is the ferment made for the white spiritual elixir.

Now we have sufficiently determined the preparation of the imperfect [metals] and the refinement of the perfect ones, so that any discerning person may fulfill their intention regarding them. Let one therefore attend to the properties of the modes of action or the composition of the Greater Elixir. For we seek to make one single substance, yet one gathered from many, united and fixed: which, when placed upon the fire, the fire does not drive out; and when melted, it may be mixed and liquefied with them, by that which is in it of an ingressive substance <note>a substance capable of penetrating the metal</note>; and be mixed with that which is of a permissive substance; and be consolidated with that which is in it

<column-break/>

of a consolidating substance, and be fixed with that which is in it of a fixative substance: and let it not be burned by those things which do not burn gold and silver: and let it bring about consolidations with a due and perfect ignition. However, do not understand this to mean it can be restored in a short time, such as a few days or hours, on the first attempt. But rather that in respect to other modern physicians, and even in respect to the operation of nature's truth: this work is indeed finished more quickly. Whence the philosopher said: "Medicine is that which anticipates a long space of time." Wherefore I say to you, that you should endure patiently, because it lingers strongly: and haste is indeed from the devil. Therefore, he who has no patience, let him take his hand from the work: because a hasty belief will hinder him. For every natural action has its own measure and a determined time, in which it is completed in a greater or lesser space. For this work, three things are necessary: patience, delay, and the fitness of the instruments: concerning which we have addressed the craftsman in the summary of our perfect mastery, in various Chapters: in which he may experiment if he has been sufficiently searched in our sayings. In these we conclude with manifest and open proof, that our stone <note>the Philosopher's Stone</note> is nothing other than a stinking spirit and living water (which we have also named "dry water") and cleansed by natural proportion and united by such a union that it cannot be separated from itself. To these a third must be added to shorten the work: that is, the thinned perfect body.

<summary>This page details the final calcination of gold and silver into "spiritual water" to act as a ferment for the Elixir, while emphasizing that the alchemical process requires extreme patience and specific natural proportions.</summary>
<keywords>calcination, salt ammoniac, gold, silver, Elixir, Philosopher's Stone, living water, alchemical patience, spiritual body</keywords>

--- Page 131 ---
<meta>This page concludes the third book of the work attributed to Geber, transitioning from the final philosophical exhortations to the printer's colophon. It maintains the theme of experimental validation found on previous pages.</meta>

# BOOK THREE

From the premises already stated, those matters in which the truth is near at hand are made clear. We are completing this work through our own true investigation, by which we are certain—and have observed through manifest experience—that all the words are true which have been written by us alone in our volumes, according to what we have seen reduced to practice through experiment and reason in these books. Indeed, those things which we have extracted with our own fingers through our experience, seen with our eyes, and touched with our hands, we have written in the summary of our perfection of the <term>mastery</term> <note>Latin: *magisterii*; refers to the "Great Work" or the successful completion of the alchemical process</note>. Therefore, let the wise craftsman study our volumes, gathering together our dispersed intentions—which we have set forth in various places so that they might not be made public to the wicked or the ignorant—and let him test what he has gathered, until through study and experimenting with the persistence of ingenious labor, he reaches total knowledge. Let him exer-

<column-break/>

-cise himself, therefore, and he shall find. Regarding the method of this investigation, moved by a deep-seated love <note>literally "from the bowels," a common medieval expression for heartfelt sincerity</note>, we consider our investigation of perfect things, and that the matter with its form is pure from the root of its mixture until its completion, without any corruption of the higher mind. Conversely, we have considered that the substance of imperfect things is one and the same everywhere: namely, <term>quicksilver</term> <note>mercury</note> and sulfur, which are pure and clean before their mixture. And through this consideration and our own practice, we have found that the corruption of imperfect things has arrived accidentally, giving a new and corrupt form to the matter.
